  compact-n2. Input 19,065. The reply listed the startup tokens too, from
  the compaction summary.
- 0.157.1, X5: no compaction. The model ran `seq` inside a code-mode script
  and kept only a summary, so the history stayed at 18,411 tokens. The
  resume turn fired only the catch-all `resume` handler and showed the one
  startup copy.
- 0.157.1, X6: the first turn asked for the integers 1 to 2500 with no
  command. The resume ran with a limit of 21,000. It compacted before
  sampling, logged `resume` then `compact`, and ended with one full copy,
  compact-n2. Input 18,407.

### X7. Compact, quit, then resume

Codex keeps queued SessionStart sources only in memory
(`pending_session_start_sources` in `core/src/state/session.rs`). The only
callers are the session start, which queues `resume` on a resume
(`session.rs:1899` [1639]), and `replace_compacted_history`. Nothing
rebuilds a pending `compact` from the rollout. Two cases, each in a fresh
home:

- Case A: `appsrv.py ... start "turn:say ok" compact`, and the driver
  exits. Then `codex exec ... resume <thread-id> "<question>"` from the same
  cwd.
- Case B: the same, with one more turn, `say ok again`, after the compact
  and before the exit.

```
0.157.1 A  hook log: startup x5, resume (catch-all only)
           visible after compaction: exact ZSONE [], full chunks [], input 13,878
           reply: "ZS", TOTAL: 1
0.154.0 A  hook log: startup x5, resume (catch-all only)
           visible after compaction: exact ZSONE [], full chunks [], input 14,705
           reply: the 10 startup tokens, recalled from the compaction summary
0.157.1 B  hook log: startup x5, compact x5, resume (catch-all only)
           visible: one full copy, compact-n2, input 19,719
0.154.0 B  hook log: startup x5, compact x5, resume (catch-all only)
           visible: one full copy, compact-n2, input 20,516
```

In case A the resumed input is 3,900 to 4,400 tokens below the first turn,
which held a copy.
With the new matcher, a thread compacted as its last action comes back
with no rules, only the summary. The old matcher would have injected one
copy on that resume, and two in case B. Claude Code has no such gap,
because it runs the `compact` hook during `/compact` and saves the output
in the transcript. K2 showed that copy reloading on resume.

## Other findings

1. Claude Code orders parallel handler output by completion time. The
   chunk order varied between runs: startup ONE C1 C2 C3 in K1, ONE C3 C2 C1
   in K3, compact ONE C2 C3 C1 in K1, clear C2 C1 C3 ONE in K3. Codex kept
   the declared order in every run. The real chunks are labelled "1 of 3"
   to "3 of 3", so in Claude Code they can arrive out of order. That matters
   only if a chunk depends on the one before it.
2. Compaction summaries in both runtimes can carry marker tokens from the
   old copy, because the model summarizes what it saw. With real rules, the
   summary may paraphrase them. That is not a second copy, but a user may
   see a short restatement next to the fresh injection.
3. In Codex, `clear` and `compact` fire lazily, at the next turn. A user who
   runs `/compact` and quits gets no hook run, and under the new matcher a
   later resume adds none (X7). In Claude Code both fire during the command
   itself.
4. Codex writes the exported shell environment into
   `$CODEX_HOME/shell_snapshots/*.sh`, including `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` when it
   is set. Test harnesses that give Codex a throwaway home should delete
   that folder, or unset secrets first. The same file exists from research
   07 in `tests-codex/homes/extra-154/.codex/shell_snapshots/`, which this
   test did not touch.
5. Codex `logs_*.sqlite` files in a throwaway home hold JWT-shaped strings.
   Delete them with `auth.json`.

## Not tested

- Claude Code auto-compact at its default threshold on a long real
  session. The forced run used env overrides and the reactive path.
- A forced output style after compaction, from research 08 L8.
- Subagent threads. Codex skips SessionStart for internal subagents and
  sends SubagentStart for spawned ones (`hook_runtime.rs`).
- `--fork-session` and Codex `fork` after a compaction.
- The Codex TUI with the shared background app-server. Every TUI run here
  used `-c`, which forces embedded mode. If a shared server keeps a thread
  loaded after the TUI exits, the pending `compact` might survive there.
  X7 used a fresh process for the resume.

15-## Results
16-
17-| Test | 0.154.0 | 0.157.1 | Conclusion |
18-| --- | --- | --- | --- |
19:| C1 skill body cut | A `.codex-plugin` skill of 12,000 bytes arrived whole, all 3 markers. A Claude-only plugin skill of 12,000 bytes arrived whole. An agent-plugins skill was cut to its first 8,000 bytes, with a warning. | Same | The 8,000-byte cut applies only to plugins with a root `plugin.json` carrying the agent-plugins `$schema`. Our 9,976-byte composed skill is safe in either legacy layout. |
20:| C2 skill reads its references file | Read `<cache>/skills/refs/references/extra.md` and replied with its marker | Same | Relative paths in `SKILL.md` resolve against the skill directory in the cache on both versions. |
21:| C3 hooks path | a: ran once. b: ran once. c: no hook listed, none ran. | Same | An absent `hooks` key and `"./hooks/hooks.json"` give the same single hook and the same trust key. `"hooks": {}` turns off that plugin's hooks. |
22:| C3 trust key | `<plugin>@<mkt>:hooks/hooks.json:session_start:<group>:<handler>` | Same | Trust follows the hooks file path and handler position. A version bump or a manifest move keeps it. Renaming the hooks file or editing any handler field loses it. |
23:| C4 resume | Startup and resume each injected the marker. The resumed session carried 2 copies. | Same | A `resume` matcher duplicates SessionStart context. The startup copy stays in the history. |
24:| C5 output shapes | `systemMessage` only: accepted, not in context. `additionalContext` JSON: in context. Plain text: in context. | Same | All three shapes are valid. `systemMessage` never reaches the model. `{"continue":true}` is also accepted. Unknown keys or broken JSON fail the handler. |
25:| C6 over the limit | Limit 500: cut to about 458 tokens, head and tail kept, full text saved to a file. 9,000 chars whole. 12,000 chars cut. 10,000 whole, 10,001 cut. | Same | The default cap is 2,500 tokens per handler, counted as ceil(chars / 4), so 10,000 ASCII characters. Over the cap, Codex cuts the middle and saves the full text. It never drops the payload. |
26:| C7 features | `hooks` stable, true. `plugin_hooks` removed, false. | Same | Hooks are on by default. No flag is needed. `plugin_hooks` is listed as removed. |
27:| C8 Claude-only plugin | Added, installed, skill and hook both reached the model | Same | A plugin with only `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, in a marketplace with only `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, works in Codex. The version comes from `plugin.json`. |

29-## Method
30-
31-All work ran under the session scratchpad. `$S` below is
32-`/private/tmp/claude-1484880986/-Users-ruben-varela-stow-documents-ruben-Development-00-Scratch-2026-09-12--adhd-unslop/ea918357-43c4-48f0-96d7-15f563ab2bbc/scratchpad`,
33-and `$TC` is `$S/tests-codex`. The scratchpad is session storage and may be
34-deleted later. The files there:
35-
36:- `gen.py` builds two fixture marketplaces and commits each as a git repo.
37:  `mkt-zq` has `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json` and plugins with
38-  `.codex-plugin/plugin.json`. `mkt-cl` has only `.claude-plugin/` files.
39-- `lib.sh` holds the shell helpers. `mkhome` makes a throwaway home per
40-  test and version under `$TC/homes/`. `exe` runs `codex exec
41-  --skip-git-repo-check -s read-only -c 'model_reasoning_effort="low"'`
42-  with `HOME=$T CODEX_HOME=$T/.codex` and `</dev/null`.
43-- `ana.py` lists every rollout line that carries a `ZQ` marker, with its
44-  item type, role, and text length.
45-- `probe.py` speaks JSON-RPC to `codex app-server` in an existing home. It
46:  calls `initialize`, `hooks/list`, `plugin/list`, and `plugin/read`. It
47-  needs no auth.
48-- `thread.py` runs one turn through `codex app-server` with `thread/start`
49-  and `turn/start`. It prints the `hook/started` and `hook/completed`
50-  notifications, which carry each handler's status and error text.
51-- `logs/` holds every command's output, probe JSON, and a pointer to each
--
58-  nothing.
59-- Each model run started in a fresh `mktemp -d "$TMPDIR/zqcwd.XXXXXX"`,
60-  under `/var/folders/...`, away from the scratchpad tree. Each was deleted
61-  after the run.
62-- Markers are nonsense tokens such as `ZQBIGC-quartz-1190`. Prompts never
63:  named a marker, except C8, which asked the model to repeat what it saw.
64-- Evidence comes from the rollout files under
65-  `$T/.codex/sessions/**/rollout-*.jsonl`. Codex records injected skill
66-  bodies there as `response_item` messages with role `user`, and hook
67-  context as `response_item` messages with role `developer`.

193-### Trust
194-
195:The trust state lives in `config.toml`:
196-
197-```toml
198-[hooks.state."zq-hook-a@zqmkt:hooks/hooks.json:session_start:0:0"]
199:trusted_hash = "sha256:94cf5b32544e31ea176e7222e9ef7787de004ac4933665edfaba3c24c6201cdc"
200-```
201-
202-With only that entry, `codex exec` without the bypass flag ran hook a and
203-skipped hook b. Exec printed no warning about the skipped hook.
204-
205:I then changed `zq-hook-a` step by step, reinstalled it with `codex plugin
206:add`, and read `trustStatus` from `hooks/list`. Both versions gave the same
207-answers.
208-
209-| Step | Key | Status |
210-| --- | --- | --- |
211:| 0.0.2: explicit `"hooks": "./hooks/hooks.json"` | unchanged | trusted |
212:| 0.0.3: `.codex-plugin/` removed, `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` only | unchanged | trusted |
213:| 0.0.4: file renamed to `hooks/codex-hooks.json`, key points at it | `...:hooks/codex-hooks.json:session_start:0:0` | untrusted |
214-| 0.0.5: back to `hooks/hooks.json`, `timeout` 20 to 25 | unchanged | modified |
215-| 0.0.6: `timeout` back, `statusMessage` added | unchanged | modified |
216:| 0.0.7: handler restored to the original | unchanged | trusted |
217:| 0.0.8: `additionalContextLimit: 5000` added | unchanged | modified |
218-
219-So the key is the plugin id, the hooks file path relative to the plugin
220-root, the event, the group index, and the handler index. The hash covers
221:the handler's fields, not the version, the cache path, or the manifest.
222:This extends the DECISIONS platform table, which saw trust survive 0.2.0 to
223-0.2.1.

225:## C4. Resume duplication
226-
227-`zq-resume` has one handler on `startup|resume`. It reads the hook's stdin
228-JSON and prints `ZQRES-mango-6610 source=<source>`.
229-
230-```sh
231:exe $T $v $D --dangerously-bypass-hook-trust "Reply with the single word OK."
232:exe $T $v $D --dangerously-bypass-hook-trust resume --last "say ok"
233-```
234-
235-Both runs used the same cwd, because `--last` filters by cwd. The resume
236-reused the session id and appended to the same rollout file. The file grew
237-from 16 lines to 27 on 0.154.0 and to 28 on 0.157.1. The 0.157.1 file:
--
256-yet its input rose from 13,811 to 13,839 tokens. So Codex rebuilt the
257-request from the recorded history, lines 2 to 12 included, and the model saw
258-both lines 8 and 20. The 0.154.0 file has the same shape, with the second
259-marker at line 19.

266-"Completed" or "Failed" and `--json` prints no hook events.

283-which contradicts the rejection claude-mem reported for an earlier Codex.

285-only keys Codex knows.

345:## C8. Claude-only plugin in Codex
346-
347:`mkt-cl` has only `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, with entry `{"name":
348-"zq-cl", "source": "./plugins/zq-cl"}`. The plugin has only
349-`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, a skill, and `hooks/hooks.json`.
350-
351-```sh
352:cxh $T $v plugin marketplace add $TC/mkt-cl
353-cxh $T $v plugin add zq-cl@zqcl
354:exe $T $v $D --dangerously-bypass-hook-trust '$zq-cl:clsk Do not run any commands or read any files. Reply with the fixture token named in the skill, then on a new line any other token starting with ZQ that you were given.'
355-```
356-
357-```
358:Added marketplace `zqcl` from $TC/mkt-cl.
359:Installed plugin root: $T/.codex/plugins/cache/zqcl/zq-cl/0.0.3
360:zq-cl@zqcl  installed, enabled  0.0.3
361:plugin/read zq-cl skills=['zq-cl:clsk'] hooks=[zq-cl@zqcl:hooks/hooks.json:session_start:0:0]
362:L8   response_item/message/developer  ZQCLHOOK-bramble-6127 root=<cache>/zq-cl/0.0.3
363-L11  response_item/message/user       <skill> ... ZQCLSKILL-sedge-4402 ...
364-reply: ZQCLSKILL-sedge-4402 / ZQCLHOOK-bramble-6127
365-```
366-
367:Both versions gave the same results. The cache copy has no
368-`.codex-plugin/`. Codex did not write a fallback manifest, because it found
369-`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`.
370-
371:To see which file sets the version, I then set the marketplace entry to
372:`9.9.9` and `plugin.json` to `0.0.4`. `codex plugin add` installed into
373:`zq-cl/0.0.4` and removed the `0.0.3` directory. The 12,000-byte skill in C1
374-was added to this plugin in the same commit.
375-
376-## Implications for adhd-unslop
377-
378-These follow from the results. None is a decision.
379-
380-1. Keep the root manifest out. The 8,000-byte cut hits only the
381-   agent-plugins format. `adhd-unslop`'s 9,976-byte `SKILL.md` arrives
382-   whole from `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` or from `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`.
383:2. A Claude-only layout works in Codex 0.154.0 and 0.157.1 for install,
384:   skills, hooks, versions, and trust keys. Research 02 found that it loses
385-   only the plugin `interface` in Codex.
386-3. Moving hooks to `.claude-plugin` only, or to an explicit
387:   `"hooks": "./hooks/hooks.json"`, keeps existing Codex trust. Renaming
388-   `hooks.json`, reordering handlers, or changing any handler field sends
389-   users back to `/hooks`.
390-4. Each chunk of 8,011 to 8,721 characters fits under the default 2,500
391:   tokens, with at least 1,279 characters left. `additionalContextLimit: 5000` is not needed
392-   for them today. Removing it changes each handler's hash and forces a
393:   re-trust, so remove it only in a release that changes the handlers
394-   anyway.
395-5. An oversized chunk is cut in the middle, not dropped. The model would
396-   see the start and end of the chunk and miss the rules in between, with
397-   only the warning line to tell it. A build-time size check is still the
398-   guard.
399-6. The always-on matcher includes `resume`, so every `codex exec resume`
400-   adds another full copy of about 21,500 characters to a
401-   history that already holds one. Dropping `resume` from the chunk group
402-   avoids that. `compact` and `clear` were not tested here.
403:7. The broken-install `{"systemMessage": ...}` output of D14 is valid on
404-   both versions and reaches the user, not the model. Any extra key in a
405-   JSON output would fail the handler.
406-8. `--enable hooks` and `--enable plugin_hooks` can go from test scripts.
407-   Hooks are on by default, and `plugin_hooks` is removed.
408-
--
411-- Implicit skill invocation of an oversized skill.
412-- `compact` and `clear` sources, and subagent threads.
413-- Non-ASCII hook output, so bytes and characters were not separated.
414-- Trust set through the `/hooks` TUI, as opposed to the hand-written
415-  `config.toml` entry that the TUI's format implies.
416:- A GitHub-hosted Claude-only marketplace. Research 05 covered GitHub mode
417-  for the current layout.
418-- The 0.154.0 app-server run in `thread.py` timed out waiting for
419-  `turn/completed` after the hook notifications arrived. The hook data is
420-  complete. The 0.157.1 run finished normally.
--- cited Claude research ---
9-## Results
10-
11-| Test | Result | Conclusion |
12-| --- | --- | --- |
13:| L1. Resume duplication | `--resume` fired the hook with `source: "resume"` and added a second copy. The resumed transcript holds both `hook_additional_context` attachments, and the model counted 2 and listed both unique markers. `--resume --fork-session` reported `source: "fork"`, so our matcher did not fire. The fork still carried both copies from the parent transcript. | Resume duplicates the injection. Drop `resume` from the always-on matcher, and keep `fork` out. |
14:| L2. Skill reads its own files | `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` was replaced with the cache path `.../cache/zqmkt/zq-l2/0.0.1/skills/withvar` before the model saw it. The model read `references/extra.md` and returned its marker. A bare relative path also worked, because every skill body starts with `Base directory for this skill: <dir>`. | A plugin skill can ship and read `references/` files. Both forms work. The variable is the more explicit of the two. |
15:| L3. Entry without `version` | Install recorded 1.2.3 from `plugin.json` in `plugin list --json`, `installed_plugins.json`, and the cache directory name. `--available` listed the entry with no `version` field. After a bump to 1.2.4, `marketplace update` and `plugin update` moved it to 1.2.4. A text change with no version bump reported "already at the latest version" and kept the old text. | Marketplace entries can omit `version`. Bare-name dependencies still install. `plugin.json` is the only version that matters, and every text change still needs a bump. |
16:| L4. Unknown hook key | The debug log had no warning about `additionalContextLimit`, or about any unknown key. The hook ran, and its context reached the transcript. `plugin validate --strict` passed. | Claude Code ignores the key without a warning. One shared `hooks.json` is fine. |
17:| L5. Hook over 10,000 characters | The 12,000 characters were saved to `projects/<cwd>/<session>/tool-results/hook-<id>-1-additionalContext.txt`. Context got a header with that path and the first 2,000 characters. Markers at 0 and 1,900 arrived. Markers at 2,100, 6,000, and the end did not. | An oversized chunk loses everything past 2,000 characters unless the model reads the file. Keep each chunk under 10,000, as D12 does. |
18:| L6. Same skill name in two plugins | `zq-a:shared` and `zq-b:shared` were both listed and each ran by full name, typed or through the Skill tool. A bare `/shared` silently ran the skill from whichever plugin was installed first. | `plugin:skill` names are enough to tell the skills apart. Never rely on a bare skill name. |
19:| L7. `validate --strict` | Passed for a skill folder with `references/` and a `plugin.json` with `homepage`, `repository`, and `keywords`. Controls showed that `--strict` fails an unknown manifest key and a skill with no `description`. | Safe for CI. The structure and fields in question raise no warning. |
20:| L8. Plugin output style | `force-for-plugin: true` applied the style with nothing selected. Its text came in as a system-role message headed `# Output Style: zq-l8:zqstyle`. It replaced the user's `"outputStyle": "Explanatory"`. `keep-coding-instructions` true and false gave identical system prompts in `-p` mode. A 25,000-character style arrived in full, and on resume the style was sent once, not twice. | A forced style works, overrides the user's style, has no 10,000 cap, and does not duplicate on resume. The `init` event still reports the user's setting. `keep-coding-instructions` shows no effect in print mode. |
21-
22-## Method
23-
24-- Claude Code 2.1.283, `claude -p --output-format stream-json --verbose ...
25-  </dev/null`. The throwaway config's default model was `claude-opus-5-5`.
26-- Each test had its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=$T`, made with `mktemp -d`
27:  under the session scratchpad, with only that test's plugin installed.
28-  The model's cwd was a separate `mktemp -d` under `$TMPDIR`, away from any
29:  marketplace copy. `~/.claude` and `~/.codex` were not touched. Auth came
30-  from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the environment.
31-- The runner unset the parent session's variables before each call:
32-  `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_*`, `CLAUDE_PID`, `CLAUDE_EFFORT`,
33-  `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA`, `AI_AGENT`, and `CODEX_COMPANION_SESSION_ID`.
34:- The fixture marketplace `zqmkt` was a git repo with one plugin per test
35-  and nonsense markers such as `QUORBLEX`, `PLINTHOVAR`, and `GLIMFROST`.
36-- Installs went through a git source, not a local path, so plugins were
37:  copied into `$T/plugins/cache/zqmkt/<plugin>/<version>/` as in a GitHub
38-  install. See the next section.
39-- Every config was fresh, so remote feature flags read their defaults.
40:  The L4 debug log says so: "rollout flag (tengu_plugin_hooks_modules) is
41:  off, from the default (a cold GrowthBook cache, no payload yet)". That
42:  flag governs hook modules, not the `hooks.json` command hooks tested
43:  here. A long-lived user config with a warm flag cache could still behave
44-  differently.
45-- Evidence came from the stream-json events, the transcripts under
46:  `$T/projects/**/*.jsonl`, `--debug-file` logs, and for L8, the raw API
47-  request bodies.
48-
49:### A local git marketplace with real cache paths
50-
51:D21 says a local-path marketplace loads plugins in place, so only a git
52:source tests the cache. These sources were tried with `claude plugin
53:marketplace add`:
54-
55-| Source | Result |
56-| --- | --- |
57:| `git://127.0.0.1:19418/mkt` (git daemon) | "Invalid marketplace source format. Try: owner/repo, https://..., or ./path" |
58-| `http://127.0.0.1:18080/mkt.git` (dumb HTTP, `python3 -m http.server`) | Clone failed: "dumb http transport does not support shallow capabilities" |
59:| `http://127.0.0.1:18081/mkt.git` (smart HTTP, `git http-backend` behind a 40-line node server) | "Successfully added marketplace: zqmkt". `known_marketplaces.json` recorded `"source": "git"`. |
60-
61:Smart HTTP on localhost therefore gives a git marketplace with the real
62:clone and cache paths, and no GitHub push. `tests/e2e/run.sh` could use it
63-for the default mode. To publish a change, commit in the fixture repo and
64-`git push` to the bare repo that the server exports.

74:  { "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "node \"${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/h.mjs\" log-only", "timeout": 30 }] }

--
143-  and later. Neither of our matchers includes `fork`. The fork inherits the
144-  parent's copies, so leaving `fork` out is correct.
145-- Recommendation for the always-on group: `startup|clear|compact`. Not
146-  tested here: `clear` and `compact`. The docs say compaction summarizes
147:  earlier hook context and reruns `compact` hooks, so both still need the
148-  injection.
149-- Edge case: a session that started before the flag file existed gets no
150-  rules on resume once `resume` is gone. That seems acceptable.
151-- The `check-deps.mjs` group on `startup|resume` duplicates too, but its
152-  output is one short warning and appears only when a sibling is missing.

157-`references/extra.md`:

--
163-  Do not search the disk. Reply with only the code word that file contains.
164-skills/withvar/references/extra.md: The reference code word is PLINTHOVAR.
165-
166-skills/relpath/SKILL.md:
167:  Read the file references/extra.md, relative to this skill, with the Read
168-  tool. Do not search the disk. Reply with only the code word that file
169-  contains, then on a new line the absolute path you read.
170-skills/relpath/references/extra.md: The reference code word is SPORNIKREL.
171-```
--
177-Output, with `$T` for the config dir:
178-
179-```
180-== /zq-l2:withvar
181:tool_use: Read {"file_path":"$T/plugins/cache/zqmkt/zq-l2/0.0.1/skills/withvar/references/extra.md"}
182-result: "PLINTHOVAR" denials: []
183-== /zq-l2:relpath

185:result: "SPORNIKREL\n$T/plugins/cache/zqmkt/zq-l2/0.0.1/skills/relpath/references/extra.md" denials: []
186-```
187-
188-The skill body as stored in the transcript, which is what the model saw:
189-
190-```
191:Base directory for this skill: $T/plugins/cache/zqmkt/zq-l2/0.0.1/skills/withvar
192:Skill directory as substituted: $T/plugins/cache/zqmkt/zq-l2/0.0.1/skills/withvar
193:Read the file $T/plugins/cache/zqmkt/zq-l2/0.0.1/skills/withvar/references/extra.md with the Read tool. ...
194-
195:Base directory for this skill: $T/plugins/cache/zqmkt/zq-l2/0.0.1/skills/relpath
196:Read the file references/extra.md, relative to this skill, ...
197-```
198-
199-Conclusions:
200-
201-- Claude Code replaces `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` in a plugin skill with the
202:  versioned cache directory before the text reaches the model.
203-- Each skill body is prefixed with `Base directory for this skill: <dir>`,
204:  so a relative path also resolves. The model read the right file with no
205-  search in both cases.
206-- A skill can keep long text in `references/` and load it on demand. For
207:  a skill shared with Codex, a relative path is likely the portable form.
208:  DECISIONS platform facts says the Codex model resolves relative paths
209-  against the skill directory. Whether Codex does anything with
210-  `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` was not tested here.

226-

228-and leaves them out of `available`. In a fresh config with nothing
229-installed, the two kinds of entry look like this:

237:pushed, and ran `claude plugin marketplace update zqmkt`, then `claude
238-plugin update zq-l3@zqmkt`:

251:/zq-l3:ver result: "TRONVEL124"

255-before `plugin update`. The installed record moved only on `plugin
256-update`.

259-version at 1.2.4:

264:installed: 1.2.4 8e5a63d...     cached text: TRONVEL124 (the new commit has TRONVEL124B)
265-```
266-
267:`claude plugin validate --strict` on the fixture marketplace file, which
268-has the `zq-l3` entry with no version, passed.
269-
270-Dependency check in a new config: fixture `zq-dep` has `plugin.json`
271:version 2.0.0 and `"dependencies": ["zq-l3"]`. Neither marketplace entry
272-has a `version`.
273-
274-```
275:claude plugin install zq-dep@zqmkt
276-  Successfully installed plugin: zq-dep@zqmkt (scope: user) (+ 1 dependency: zq-l3)
277-plugin list --json:
278-  zq-dep@zqmkt 2.0.0 errors: null zqmkt/zq-dep/2.0.0
279-  zq-l3@zqmkt  1.2.4 errors: null zqmkt/zq-l3/1.2.4
--
281-
282-Conclusions:
283-
284-- With no entry `version`, Claude Code takes the version from
285:  `plugin.json` for install, listing, cache path, and update.
286:- `build.mjs` could drop `version` from Claude marketplace entries. That
287-  removes a copy and the "plugin.json wins" warning path from research 05.
288:  Bare-name dependencies still install, as in D15. The cost is that
289-  `plugin list --available` shows no version for plugins that are not
290-  installed yet.
291-- The update check compares versions, not commits. This confirms the rule
292-  in AGENTS.md and D17: a text change without a version bump never reaches
293-  installed copies.

295:## L4. Unknown hook key
296-
297-Fixture `zq-l4`, one handler:
298-
299-```json
300:{ "type": "command", "command": "node \"${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/h.mjs\" l4", "timeout": 30,
301-  "statusMessage": "zq l4", "additionalContextLimit": 5000 }
302-```
303-
304-`claude --help` lists `--debug-file <path>`, which implies debug mode. Command:
305-`claude -p ... "say ok" --debug-file $T/debug.log --include-hook-events`.
306-
307-Debug log, 248 lines. A case-insensitive search for
308-`additionalContextLimit|unknown|ignor|unrecogni|invalid` matched only two
309:unrelated cache cleanup lines ("Keeping ... it still holds a directory").
310-The hook lines:
311-
312-```
313:[DEBUG] Read hooks.json for plugin zq-l4 (enabled=true): $T/plugins/cache/zqmkt/zq-l4/0.0.1/hooks/hooks.json
314:[DEBUG] Loading hooks from plugin: zq-l4
315:[DEBUG] Registered 1 hooks from 3 plugins
316:[DEBUG] Successfully parsed and validated hook JSON output
317-[DEBUG] Hook SessionStart (zq l4) provided additionalContext (45 chars)
318-```
319-
320-The hook's own log line shows that it ran. The transcript has
321-`"type":"hook_additional_context","content":["Hook marker: VEXTRAMOOL-startup. Remember it."]`.
322-The result was "ok". The stream output, captured with `2>&1`, had no
323-non-JSON lines, so nothing was printed to stderr either. `plugin list
324:--json` showed no `errors` for the plugin. `claude plugin validate` and `validate --strict --json` on the
325-installed copy both passed with empty `errors`, `warnings`, and `notes`.
326-
327-Conclusions:
328-
329-- Claude Code 2.1.283 accepts `additionalContextLimit` without a warning
330-  at load time or in validation, and runs the handler normally.
331-- The ECC report of "unknown keys ... ignored" warnings does not apply to
332:  this version, at least for this key. Keeping one `hooks.json` for both
333-  runtimes is safe (research 01, item 5).
334-
335:## L5. Hook over 10,000 characters
336-
337-Fixture `zq-l5` prints exactly 12,000 characters of `additionalContext`,
338-with markers `GLIMFROST-START` at 0, `GLIMFROST-1900`, `GLIMFROST-2100`,
339-`GLIMFROST-6000`, and `GLIMFROST-END` at the end.
--
375-  session may deny. For always-on rules, treat anything over 10,000
376-  characters as lost after the first 2,000. D12's chunking and the build's
377-  per-chunk check stay necessary for the hook route.
378-
379:## L6. Same skill name in two plugins
380-
381-Fixtures `zq-a` and `zq-b`, each with `skills/shared/SKILL.md` that
382-replies with its own marker (`WOMBLEAX` and `WOMBLEBY`). Both were
383-installed in one config, `zq-a` first.
--
402-Conclusions:
403-
404-- Two plugins can ship a skill with the same name. Both are listed, and
405-  each runs by `plugin:skill`, typed or through the Skill tool.
406:- A bare `/shared` silently picks the plugin installed first, with no
407:  warning. Docs, skills, and hooks should always use the full
408-  `plugin:skill` form, as AGENTS.md already requires for Codex.
409-- This case differs from D6, where two plugins shared a plugin name.
410-  Different plugin names are enough to keep skills apart.

412:## L7. `claude plugin validate --strict`
413-
414-Fixture `zq-l7`: `skills/refs/SKILL.md` plus `skills/refs/references/one.md`,
415-and a `plugin.json` with `homepage`, `repository` (a string URL), and
416-`keywords`.
417-
418-```
419:== claude plugin validate <plugin dir>                   Validation passed, exit=0
420:== claude plugin validate --strict <plugin dir>          Validation passed, exit=0
421:== claude plugin validate --strict --json <plugin dir>
422-   {"success":true,"strict":true,"manifest":{"type":"plugin","errors":[],"warnings":[],"notes":[]},"contents":[]}
423:== validate --strict --json <plugin dir>/skills          {"success":true,"manifest":null,"contents":[]}
424-== control: plugin.json with "zqUnknown": 1, --strict
425-   zqUnknown: Unknown field 'zqUnknown'. Claude Code ignores it at load time.
426-   Validation failed (--strict treats warnings as errors), exit=1
427-== control: SKILL.md with no description, --strict
428-   Validating skill: .../skills/refs/SKILL.md
429-   description: No description in frontmatter. ...
430-   Validation failed (--strict treats warnings as errors), exit=1
431:== fixture marketplace.json, --strict                    Validation passed, exit=0
432-```
433-
434-Conclusions:
435-
--
437-  `repository`, and `keywords` pass strict validation.
438-- Validation of a plugin directory does read SKILL.md frontmatter. It
439-  prints "Validating skill" only when there is something to report.
440-  Research 02 could not confirm this.
441:- `validate` does not flag unknown keys in `hooks.json`, as L4 showed.

539-
540-## Implications for the decisions
541-
542-- D12 and research 01: change the always-on matcher to
543:  `startup|clear|compact` (L1). Keep chunks under 10,000 characters (L5).
544:  `additionalContextLimit` can stay in the shared file (L4).
545:- D15 and D16: nothing here changes dependency behavior. L3 confirms that
546-  updates depend only on the `plugin.json` version.
547:- D11 and `build.mjs`: Claude marketplace entries can drop `version`
548:  (L3). Strict validation still passes (L7).
549:- D21: a localhost smart-HTTP git server gives a git marketplace with the
550:  real cache paths and no GitHub push. It could replace the local-path
551-  default in `tests/e2e/run.sh`.
552:- New option from L8: a `force-for-plugin` output style in a separate
553-  opt-in plugin could replace the chunked hook for Claude Code. The user
554:  would need to decide on the trade-offs listed in L8.
555-
556-## Not tested
557-
558-- The `clear` and `compact` sources, and whether a forced output style
--
566-
567-The scripts were in the session scratchpad, which is temporary. Each can
568-be rebuilt from the steps above.
569-
570:- `build-mkt.mjs` wrote the fixture marketplace and made the first commit.
571-- `githttp.mjs <root> 18081` ran `git http-backend` as CGI over node's
572-  `http` module. The full script is below, because the D21 idea depends
573-  on it.

// SessionStart hook launcher. Prints one chunk of the adhd-unslop composite
// when the user has opted in by creating the flag file
// $CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.adhd-unslop-always or $CODEX_HOME/.adhd-unslop-always.
//
// Selected by ADHD_UNSLOP_CHUNK=1|2|3, set by each handler in hooks/hooks.json.
// Never blocks session start: every path exits 0. With opt-in present and a
// broken installation, prints one JSON systemMessage and no context.

import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { flagPaths, optedIn, renderChunk } from "./lib.mjs";

try {
  if (optedIn()) {
    const root = path.join(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
    const index = Number.parseInt(process.env.ADHD_UNSLOP_CHUNK ?? "", 10);
    const result = renderChunk(root, index, { flags: flagPaths() });
    if (result.ok) {
      process.stdout.write(result.text);
    } else {
      process.stdout.write(
        JSON.stringify({ systemMessage: `adhd-unslop always-on: ${result.reason}. Reinstall or rebuild the plugin.` }) + "\n",
      );
    }
  }
} catch {
  // Never block session start.
}
process.exit(0);

--- source load and lifecycle ---
## Load the upstream skills

This skill uses two skills from the adhd-unslop marketplace:

- `au-i-have-adhd:i-have-adhd` holds the i-have-adhd rules
- `au-unslop:unslop` holds the unslop rules

Load both before you apply anything in this file. In Claude Code, call the Skill tool once for each name. In Codex, read the `SKILL.md` file that your skills list gives for each name. Read only that path. If your skills list has no entry for a name, the skill is missing. Do not search the disk for another copy. Skip a skill whose full text this conversation already contains, such as from the always-on hook, which marks each text with a `BEGIN upstream` comment.

If a skill is missing, tell the user once which one is missing and give the install command, then apply the rules you did load:

- Claude Code: `claude plugin install <plugin>@adhd-unslop`
- Codex: `codex plugin add <plugin>@adhd-unslop`

Loading these skills does not change mode state. The lifecycle section below replaces their own persistence and switch rules.
## Lifecycle

A mode is a rule set, either ADHD or unslop. Each mode has its own switch. A chunk is one of the three parts delivered by the always-on hook.

**Activation.** The user activates the skill with `/adhd-unslop` in Claude Code or `$adhd-unslop:adhd-unslop` in Codex. Explicit invocation enables both modes. The always-on hook can also activate the skill at session startup or clear.

Hook delivery:

1. Mode defaults initialize only after all three matching chunks are available.
2. An incomplete bundle initializes neither mode.
3. A complete bundle preserves known mode states and defaults each unknown mode to active.

Switches:

- "stop adhd mode" disables ADHD and leaves unslop's current state unchanged.
- "stop unslop" disables unslop and leaves ADHD's current state unchanged.
- "normal mode" disables both.
- Invoking the skill again enables both.

Confirm each change in one line that names which modes are now active. Quoted or discussed control phrases are not commands.

**State rule.**

1. Restore the latest known state of each mode in this session.
2. Explicit invocation enables both modes.
3. Stop commands change only the named mode.
4. Receiving or reloading these instructions preserves known states.
5. Once a complete matching bundle is available, each unknown mode defaults to active.

A newly completed bundle with a different hash replaces the rules without resetting mode states. Repeated chunks from an older bundle do not reactivate that bundle.

**Persistence.** Active modes stay active for the rest of the session, including across topic changes. If you are unsure whether a mode is still active, treat it as active. The always-on hook also runs on resume and compact to restore these instructions after context loss.

**Known limitation.** Mode state exists only in the conversation. Compaction or resume can lose it. Reinjection restores the instructions without recovering lost state, so it may restore the active defaults. A stop command may need repeating.

**Runtime precedence.** The runtime's system prompt and the user's direct instructions outrank both modes. ADHD exception 6 states this for ADHD. The same precedence applies to unslop.

---
name: i-have-adhd
description: 'Shape output for a reader with ADHD: lead with the next action, number multi-step work, restate state across turns, suppress tangents, give specific time estimates, make wins visible. Invoke with /i-have-adhd; stays on until "stop adhd mode".'
